- [x] **The room outbox claim is a real queue, and its crash recovery no longer retries for ever**
  - Date: 2026-10-18
  - Session: CC-20261001-q7m4
  - What changed: `backend/src/services/communityRooms/__tests__/roomOutboxClaimRace.integration.test.ts` (new, 8 tests) proves the claim; `roomOutboxService.ts` bounds crash recovery (counts the crash as an attempt, dead-letters when they are spent) and exports `recoverStaleProcessing` so that bound can be observed; register updated.
  - Verification: **ROOM OUTBOX CLAIM RACE, against real Postgres 16 on port 55437, 8/8 passing after the last edit.** Two concurrent drains deliver six events exactly once (six distinct ids, nothing left behind); surplus drains come back EMPTY rather than blocking; an event inside its backoff window is not claimable; five racing emits produce ONE row; and a pool guard stops the suite becoming a no-op on a single connection. **MUTATION RUN BOTH WAYS:** deleting `lock` + `skipLocked` from `claimBatch` delivers the six events **TWELVE** times (every event claimed by both drains); deleting `attempts + 1` from recovery leaves the row still claimable after **twenty** crash cycles instead of dead-lettering after exactly six. Siblings re-run green: `sessionClassNotesService`, `broadcastCommandService`, the four `communityRooms` suites and `modelTimestampTypeLies` - 74 passed. Verified it SKIPS AND SAYS WHY without `DATABASE_URL` (8 skipped, reason printed). Container removed, none left behind.
  - Notes: **THIS IS THE FIRST OF THE SEVEN LOCKS THAT ACTUALLY RUNS.** `COMMUNITY_ROOMS_ENABLED` is `true` in production (queried by name), the drain is scheduled every minute, and the table holds **284 delivered events** from 2026-07-22 to 2026-10-17 - 58 of them RSVP reminders that post into a real room. The register called this lock "covered by asserting its SQL"; in fact `skipLocked: true` appears exactly once in the repository, in the service, and nothing tested it. **THE MISSING BOUND:** `recoverStale` reset stale `processing` rows without counting the attempt, and `attempts` only incremented in the drain's catch block, which a crashed worker never reaches - so a poison event was reclaimable every minute for ever and could never reach `max_attempts`. Prohibited by CLAUDE.md, and reachable on any deploy that interrupts a drain. **ALSO MEASURED:** production's highest `attempts` ever is **0**, so the retry, backoff and dead-letter branches have never executed there - three months of green is three months of the happy path. **AND A FINDING FOR THE OPEN DDL DECISION:** `sync()` builds `id`, `created_at` and `updated_at` as NOT NULL with **no default**, where production's hand-written DDL defaults them (`gen_random_uuid()`, `now()`), so a `sync()`-created table can only be written through Sequelize. Found the hard way - three failing runs, and the first two causes I proposed were both wrong until I read `\d` on both tables. **TWO REMAIN:** the enrollment row lock and the telemetry anchor.

  - Date: 2026-10-18

  - Verification: **TELEMETRY ANCHOR RACE, against real Postgres 16 on port 55438, 5/5 passing after the last edit**, with both tables built from the PRODUCTION DDL (`BROADCAST_TELEMETRY_STATEMENTS`) rather than hand-written copies. Two tabs beating in the same instant credit ten seconds ONCE; **three tabs credit the same ten seconds as one** (the docblock's own claim); a paused beat does not advance the anchor; **positive control** - the same read-then-credit without the lock bills ten seconds as twenty. **MUTATIONS BOTH WAYS:** deleting `FOR UPDATE` from the anchor read credits 20 instead of 10; reverting the cast fails 4 of 5 and prints the Postgres type error. The defect itself was proven by running the service's own logged statement against the production DDL in psql: `column "last_credited_at" is of type timestamp with time zone but expression is of type text`. Siblings re-run green: all 21 broadcast suites, **378 passed**, 5 skipped (this suite without `DATABASE_URL`). Container removed, none left behind.
  - Notes: **THE LOCK WAS FINE; THE STATEMENT AROUND IT COULD NEVER RUN.** An untyped `CASE WHEN :playing THEN :now ELSE NULL END` in a VALUES list resolves to `text`, which Postgres refuses to put in a `timestamptz` column - two sites, `people.last_credited_at` and `sessions.last_playing_at`. The DO UPDATE branches were already fine because their ELSE branch IS the column. Shipped 2026-10-09 in `adf97375`. **WHY NINE DAYS OF SILENCE:** the catch logged only `error_class` (the same line a dropped connection produces), the write is best-effort by design, and its unit suite is **13 green tests with `sequelize` mocked** - honest about being a mock, but nothing else ever parsed the SQL. Every test in the new suite asserts the call returned `true`, and that assertion is what caught it. **SCOPE, CAREFULLY:** latent, not active - nothing in `frontend/src` posts to `POST .../:id/heartbeat`, both production tables are empty, and the one occurrence that went live (2026-10-15) produced no row. **What I cannot claim** is whether a beat was ever attempted: the backend container had been up about an hour, so its logs cannot reach that date and the absence of `broadcast_beat_write_failed` proves nothing. **A HOLE IN THE LOCK:** on a first-ever beat the row does not exist, so `FOR UPDATE` locks nothing and two tabs are not serialised at all - harmless only because `creditSeconds(null)` is 0, a fact in a different file. Both halves are now pinned in one test. **TWO SELF-INFLICTED DETOURS, WORTH RECORDING:** I put backticks around a word in a SQL comment inside a JS template literal, which terminated the string and broke the module (the existing unit suite diagnosed it in one run - the integration suite could not, because **jest renders a Sequelize error as an EMPTY failure body here**, five blank failures and no message; every database call in the new suite is now wrapped to say what broke). **ONE REMAINS:** the enrollment row lock.
